## Part D: the shell-by-shell reload (look)
The same pose mechanism as the magazine guns, but the middle repeats once per shell.
- The gun leaves rest for its reload pose during the first shell's first share, holds it while shells go in, and eases back to rest after the last shell (that last ease may run a little past the gameplay end of the reload, like a cancelled reload's blend; the gun must be able to fire when gameplay says so).
- The reload pose must show the loading port: it is on the underside, in front of the trigger guard, so the gun rolls and rises until its underside faces the camera.
- Per shell: the shell (the `Magazine` part) appears below the view, rises to the end of the `MagazineOut` path, slides in along it to rest, fastest at the end, and a short bump seats it. Then it is hidden until the next one. At rest, outside a reload, the shell is hidden (it is inside the port).
- The left hand follows each shell: down to fetch, up with it, the push. The right hand follows the gun as it does today.
- A stopped or cancelled reload must not snap: shell hidden at once, gun blends to rest in about 0.12 s, as today.
- The dev pin must be able to hold one shell's cycle still at a given progress for the smoke.
- Keep it one code path where that is natural: the per-shell cycle should reuse the existing beat helpers rather than copy them.

- `Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/Core/GBStatNames.h`: one new stat name
- `Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/UI/GBHUD.cpp`: `DrawHands` and the reserve label of the ammo panel only

 925: void AGBGun::UpdateViewModel(float DeltaSeconds)
 926: {
 927: 	const FGBGunModelArt& Art = *ModelArt;
 928: 
 929: 	// Trigger pull: 0 to full pitch and back over TriggerPullSeconds (linear in, linear out). A new shot restarts it.
 930: 	if (VMTrigger && TriggerPullElapsed >= 0.f)
 931: 	{
 932: 		TriggerPullElapsed += DeltaSeconds;
 933: 		if (TriggerPullElapsed >= TriggerPullSeconds)
 934: 		{
 935: 			TriggerPullElapsed = -1.f;
 936: 			VMTrigger->SetRelativeRotation(FRotator::ZeroRotator);
 937: 		}
 938: 		else
 939: 		{
 940: 			const float Half = TriggerPullSeconds * 0.5f;
 941: 			const float Frac = TriggerPullElapsed < Half
 942: 				? TriggerPullElapsed / Half
 943: 				: 1.f - (TriggerPullElapsed - Half) / Half;
 944: 			VMTrigger->SetRelativeRotation(FRotator(TriggerPullPitch * Frac, 0.f, 0.f));
 945: 		}
 946: 	}
 947: 
 948: 	// Shot kick and muzzle flash. The step is capped so a long frame still shows each one for at least one frame.
 949: 	const float EffectStep = FMath::Min(DeltaSeconds, MaxEffectStep);
 950: 	if (KickElapsed >= 0.f)
 951: 	{
 952: 		KickElapsed += EffectStep;
 953: 		if (KickElapsed >= KickSeconds)
 954: 		{
 955: 			KickElapsed = -1.f;
 956: 		}
 957: 	}
 958: 	if (ReloadBlendElapsed >= 0.f)
 959: 	{
 960: 		ReloadBlendElapsed += DeltaSeconds;
 961: 		if (ReloadBlendElapsed >= ReloadBlendLength)
 962: 		{
 963: 			ReloadBlendElapsed = -1.f;
 964: 		}
 965: 	}
 966: 	// Pump timer: runs for PumpHomeShare of the fire interval, then the pump rests. No pump while a reload runs.
 967: 	if (PumpElapsed >= 0.f)
 968: 	{
 969: 		if (bReloading)
 970: 		{
 971: 			PumpElapsed = -1.f;
 972: 		}
 973: 		else
 974: 		{
 975: 			PumpElapsed += DeltaSeconds;

 977: 			{

 979: 			}
 980: 		}
 981: 	}

 986: 	{

 989: 		{
 990: 			HideMuzzleFlash();
 991: 		}
 992: 	}

1015: 							? FMath::Clamp(Active.TimeRemaining / Active.TotalDuration, 0.f, 1.f)

1018: 						// centres ((i + 0.5) / cells). Adding half a cell lights ceil(Left x cells) cells, so the

 621: void AGBPlayerCharacter::HandlePrecisionRecovery(AActor* Target, const FGBDamageInfo& Info, float DamageDealt)
 622: {
 623: 	(void)DamageDealt;
 624: 	if (!IsValid(Target) || Target == this || !Info.bHeadshot || Info.Instigator.Get() != this)
 625: 	{
 626: 		return;
 627: 	}
 628: 	if (!Stats || !Attributes || IsDead())
 629: 	{
 630: 		return;
 631: 	}
 632: 	// Only a blow that killed counts: a head hit that does not kill never heals.
 633: 	const UGBAttributeComponent* TargetAttributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(Target);
 634: 	if (!TargetAttributes || !TargetAttributes->IsDead())
 635: 	{
 636: 		return;
 637: 	}
 638: 	const int32 Rank = FMath::Max(0, FMath::RoundToInt(Stats->GetStat(GBStat::PrecisionRecovery, 0.f)));
 639: 	if (Rank <= 0)
 640: 	{
 641: 		return;
 642: 	}
 643: 	const UGBDataSubsystem* Data = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(this);
 644: 	const float HealFraction = Data ? Data->GetGameValue(GBRecovery::HealFraction, 0.03f) : 0.03f;

 646: 		Data ? Data->GetGameValue(GBRecovery::CooldownBase, 7.f) : 7.f,

	const UGBDataSubsystem* Data = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(this);
	const FGBItemDefinition* Def = Data ? Data->FindItem(ItemId) : nullptr;
	if (!Def)
	{
		UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("AddItem: unknown item '%s'."), *ItemId.ToString());
		return 0;
	}

	if (Def->Type == EGBItemType::Ammo)
	{
		UGBEquipmentComponent* Equipment = GetOwner() ? GetOwner()->FindComponentByClass<UGBEquipmentComponent>() : nullptr;
		if (!Equipment)
		{
			return 0;
		}
		const float* PerItem = Def->Stats.Find(GBStat::SpareMagazines);
		const int32 MagazinesPerItem = FMath::Max(1, PerItem ? FMath::RoundToInt(*PerItem) : 1);
		const int32 Magazines = Count * MagazinesPerItem;
		// A full gun takes nothing, so the pickup stays on the floor; a nearly-full one keeps the remainder.
		const int32 Added = (Magazines > 0) ? Equipment->AddSpareMagazines(Magazines) : 0;
		return UsedAmmoItems(Added, MagazinesPerItem);
	}

	if (Def->MaxStack <= 0)
	{
		UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("AddItem: item '%s' has MaxStack %d; treating it as 1."), *ItemId.ToString(), Def->MaxStack);
	}
	const int32 MaxStack = FMath::Max(1, Def->MaxStack);
	const int32 Current = GetCount(ItemId);
	const int32 Taken = FMath::Clamp(MaxStack - Current, 0, Count);
	if (Taken <= 0)
	{
		return 0;
	}

	Items.Add(ItemId, Current + Taken);
	if (QuickUseItem.IsNone() && Def->bQuickUse)
		// Owner's rule (2026-11-12): a weapon never replaces the one in hand and nothing is left on the floor.
		// A new weapon goes to its slot if that is empty, otherwise it is stored (equipped later from the inventory).
		UGBEquipmentComponent* Equipment = Player->GetEquipment();
		const UGBDataSubsystem* Data = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(Player);
		const FGBWeaponDefinition* Def = Data ? Data->FindWeapon(InOutDrop.WeaponId) : nullptr;
		if (!Equipment || !Def)
		{
			return false;
		}
		const bool bGun = Def->Slot == EGBHeldSlot::Gun;
		if (Equipment->OwnsWeapon(InOutDrop.WeaponId))
		{
			// A copy of a gun already owned is worth spare magazines if that gun is in the slot; otherwise it is just used up.
			if (bGun && Equipment->IsWeaponEquipped(InOutDrop.WeaponId))
			{
				const UGBDataSubsystem* GameData = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(Player);
				const int32 Magazines = GameData ? FMath::Max(0, FMath::RoundToInt(GameData->GetGameValue(GBPickupKey::DuplicateGunMagazines, 1.f))) : 1;
				Equipment->AddSpareMagazines(Magazines);
			}
		}
		else
		{
			Equipment->GiveWeapon(InOutDrop.WeaponId, true);
			if (!Equipment->OwnsWeapon(InOutDrop.WeaponId))
			{
				return false;
			}
		}
		bOutAllTaken = true;
		return true;
	}
	}

	if (!FlagOnPickup.IsNone())
	{
		if (UGBProgressSubsystem* Progress = UGBProgressSubsystem::Get(this))
		{
			Progress->SetFlag(FlagOnPickup);
		}
	}

	if (bWeaponDrop)
	{
		const FString Name = GetDropDisplayName(this, Before);
		const UGBEquipmentComponent* Equipment = Player->GetEquipment();
		FString Message;
		if (bOwnedBefore)
		{
			FGBAmmoState AmmoAfter;
			const bool bGotMagazine = bHadAmmo && Equipment && Equipment->GetWeaponAmmo(Before.WeaponId, AmmoAfter)
				&& AmmoAfter.SpareMagazines > AmmoBefore.SpareMagazines;
			Message = bGotMagazine ? FString::Printf(TEXT("%s: +1 magazine"), *Name) : FString::Printf(TEXT("%s: already owned"), *Name);
		}
		else if (Equipment && Equipment->IsWeaponEquipped(Before.WeaponId))
		{
			Message = FString::Printf(TEXT("Picked up %s"), *Name);
		}
		else
		{

# CLAUDE.md (proyecto de la Epic AI MegaJam 2026)

Juego para la Epic AI MegaJam 2026, en **Unreal Engine 5.9.0** (CL 60351404). La jam va del 12 nov 00:00 EST al **15 nov 20:59 EST**; pasada esa hora no se aceptan entregas. **Tema (anunciado el 11 nov): DIRECTOR'S CUT** â€” "Your vision, your edits, your final cut": la mecÃ¡nica que quedÃ³ fuera del alcance, la idea archivada; y dirigir agentes de IA tambiÃ©n cuenta (fijar la visiÃ³n, delegar, decidir quÃ© entra al corte final).

## Reglas duras
- **La bitÃ¡cora lo es todo** (objetivo: el premio Glass Box). Lee `Devlog/README.md` antes de trabajar.
  - Claude Code queda registrado solo por los hooks de usuario (`Tools/Devlog/Install-DevlogHooks.ps1`). No los desactives y no borres `Devlog/.jamroot`.
  - Cualquier otro agente o CLI se lanza SOLO con `Tools/Devlog/Invoke-LoggedAgent.ps1` (con `-Shell cmd` si el comando usa `<`).
  - El Editor se cierra SOLO con `Tools/Jam/Stop-EditorMcp.ps1`, que ademÃ¡s recoge su log.
  - Al cerrar el dÃ­a: `Tools/Devlog/New-DevlogDay.ps1`.
- **Nada de cÃ³digo ni assets de proyectos anteriores** (en particular, nada de Panthelia). Todo se crea durante la jam o estÃ¡ declarado en `Devlog/PRE_JAM_DECLARATION.md`.
- **Assets de terceros:** solo con licencia vÃ¡lida, y cada uno anotado en la entrada del dÃ­a (nombre, fuente, licencia).
- **Gameplay:** tiene que ir mÃ¡s allÃ¡ de las plantillas de Epic, y no puede ser una extensiÃ³n de un tutorial oficial de Epic. Tiene que mostrar el tema de la jam.
- **Todo el contenido del juego va en INGLÃ‰S** (regla 7(i) de las Official Rules): textos en pantalla, audio y voces, el video y los datos de la entrega. La bitÃ¡cora (Glass Box) se escribe **en inglÃ©s desde el inicio** (decisiÃ³n del dueÃ±o, 2026-11-10): entradas `Devlog/days/*.md`, pies de `media/`, `PRE_JAM_DECLARATION.md`, `PREJAM_PREP.md`, y tambiÃ©n los **mensajes de commit**, porque el borrador diario los lista. Los registros crudos (`Devlog/raw`) y los prompts del equipo pueden estar en espaÃ±ol; si se citan en una entrada, se aÃ±ade la traducciÃ³n.
- **Contenido apto para Teen (13+).** Sin marcas ni logos ajenos; el Ãºnico permitido es el logo de Unreal Engine, segÃºn sus guÃ­as de marca.
- **Historial de git limpio y completo:** Epic puede pedir el historial de control de versiones y los materiales preparados antes de la jam para verificar.
- **No debe requerir software adicional para jugarse** (por ejemplo, nada de un servidor local de LLM).
- **Respuestas** al equipo en espaÃ±ol.

## Flujo
- Prioridad: un juego jugable y empaquetado cada dÃ­a, no funcionalidades a medias.
- **Empaquetar:** `Tools/Jam/Package-Jam.ps1 -Project <uproject> -ZipName <Equipo>_<Juego>`. Hazlo al final de CADA dÃ­a, no solo el Ãºltimo.
- **Editor con MCP:**
  - abrir con `Tools/Jam/Start-EditorMcp.ps1 -Project <uproject> -Port 8059`;
  - probar con `Tools/Jam/Test-UnrealMcp.ps1`;
  - cerrar con `Tools/Jam/Stop-EditorMcp.ps1` (nunca matar el proceso).
  - ReciÃ©n abierto, el Editor responde "slow task" durante un rato: espera y reintenta.
- **PIE (Play In Editor) lo hace SIEMPRE un humano.** Los agentes de IA no lanzan PIE (trabajan muy mal con Ã©l): compilan, empaquetan o corren tests y luego piden a una persona que pruebe.
- **MCP de Unreal:** descubrimiento progresivo (`list_toolsets` â†’ `describe_toolsets` â†’ `call_tool`). No inventes nombres de tools.
- **Commits** pequeÃ±os y frecuentes, con mensajes claros: el borrador del dÃ­a los lista.
- **Push automÃ¡tico:** cada commit se sube enseguida a `origin main`, sin preguntar (dueÃ±o, 2026-11-12: los agentes de las otras mÃ¡quinas tienen que ver la repo en todo momento). Esto prevalece sobre el "nunca push salvo que se pida" de la regla global. Nunca force-push.
- **Implementador:** por defecto, la implementaciÃ³n la hace **Muse Spark 1.3 Contributor en High** a travÃ©s de OpenCode (`opencode-go/muse-spark-1.3-contributor#high`, ya fijado en `opencode.jsonc`). Excepciones solo por decisiÃ³n del arquitecto o de quien estÃ© de turno: cambios pequeÃ±os que haga directamente el orquestador, o una tarea demasiado compleja para Muse que se le da a Opus u otro modelo. Si se hace una excepciÃ³n, se anota el motivo en la entrada del dÃ­a. OpenCode se lanza SOLO con `Tools/Devlog/Invoke-LoggedAgent.ps1`.

## AutonomÃ­a del arquitecto
- El **arquitecto** es el chat de Claude Code que estÃ© implementando en ese momento. Su fuente de verdad del diseÃ±o es `Design/GDD.md`.
- **Las decisiones tÃ©cnicas y de ingenierÃ­a las toma el arquitecto, sin consultar.** Nadie del equipo es programador: no se pregunta por arquitectura de cÃ³digo, C++ o Blueprints, estructura de clases, plugins, herramientas, formatos de datos, optimizaciÃ³n ni nada parecido. Se decide, se ejecuta y se anota en la bitÃ¡cora (decisiÃ³n y motivo, que tambiÃ©n suma para Glass Box).
- Si falta un dato de diseÃ±o (gameplay, arte, historia), el arquitecto **no se detiene**: elige un valor razonable y provisional, lo marca en el GDD como provisional y sigue. Solo pregunta al dueÃ±o cuando una decisiÃ³n de diseÃ±o cambie de verdad la visiÃ³n del juego, y lo hace en una sola pregunta, sin parar el resto del trabajo.
- El tiempo manda: entre dos opciones correctas, gana la que antes deja un build jugable.

## Tiempo de iteraciÃ³n (el tiempo premia en este proyecto)
- **Plugins:** el proyecto se crea con los plugins que ya trae el motor por defecto **desactivados si no se usan**, porque alargan el arranque y la compilaciÃ³n. Se dejan solo los que el juego o el flujo necesitan (el MCP, Enhanced Input, el de DLSS si se usa, Paper2D si se usa para sprites, etc.). Se mide el arranque del Editor antes y despuÃ©s.
- **Compilar poco:** cambios solo en `.cpp` con Live Coding; build completo solo cuando cambian cabeceras, `UPROPERTY`/`UFUNCTION` o mÃ³dulos. Una compilaciÃ³n o corrida a la vez por mÃ¡quina.
- **No correr la suite entera en cada cambio:** en cada cambio, solo el filtro anclado de lo que se tocÃ³. La suite completa (por debajo de 30 s) una vez antes de empaquetar al final del dÃ­a.
- **Arranque del Editor:** mapa inicial casi vacÃ­o, `bEnableUncontrolledChangelists=False` y, en corridas sin ventana, `-unattended -nopause -nosplash -NullRHI -nosound -nocrashreports` (detalles en `Devlog/PREJAM_PREP.md`, "Where the time really went").
- **Prueba automÃ¡tica (smoke) filtrada mientras se itera** (dueÃ±o, 2026-11-13: sacar a cada momento una ventana que corre el juego entero es perder el tiempo si no se estÃ¡ tocando todo):
  - mientras se trabaja en una parte, se corre solo esa parte y sin ventana: `Tools/Jam/Smoke-Jam.ps1 -Only <Paso> -Offscreen` (por ejemplo `-Only Entity`, unos 20 s frente a los 95 s de la completa). Si la parte aÃºn no tiene filtro, se aÃ±ade su paso a `gb.Smoke <Prefijo>`;
  - mientras se itera, se compila solo el target del Editor;
  - el `Smoke-Jam.ps1` completo y `Build-Jam.ps1 -Target Glyphbound` se corren **una sola vez, al final**, antes del commit o del empaquetado;
  - todo encargo a un implementador (Muse, Sonnet u otro) incluye esta regla.
- Comandos largos en segundo plano, sin bucles de espera.

## Tests y verificaciÃ³n
Reglas que salen de lo que nos costÃ³ horas en el proyecto anterior. El porquÃ© y las cifras estÃ¡n en `Devlog/PREJAM_PREP.md`, secciÃ³n "Lessons from our previous project: tests and verification time"; lÃ©ela antes de escribir un test o una herramienta de Editor.
1. **Pocos tests, y solo de lÃ³gica pura** que sea fÃ¡cil de romper y difÃ­cil de ver jugando (puntuaciÃ³n, guardado, mÃ¡quinas de estado, generadores, matemÃ¡ticas). Toda la suite por debajo de 30 s. Lo demÃ¡s se verifica jugando el juego empaquetado.
2. **Verde o rojo.** Nada de listas de "fallos conocidos", tests excluidos ni catÃ¡logos. Un test en rojo se arregla o se borra el mismo dÃ­a.
3. **Cada test tiene que poder fallar:** antes de darlo por bueno, pregÃºntate quÃ© implementaciÃ³n incorrecta lo pasarÃ­a igual. No escribas tests que copian constantes, miran el texto del cÃ³digo o comprueban que un campo existe.
4. **Corridas baratas:** filtros anclados (`^Game.Save.`, con `^` y punto final), varios filtros unidos con `+` en una sola corrida, y un mapa inicial del Editor casi vacÃ­o (no el nivel de juego). El nombre de un test nunca puede ser tambiÃ©n la carpeta de otro (`A.B` junto a `A.B.C`): Unreal deja de correr `A.B` sin avisar.
5. **Mide antes de optimizar:** arranque del Editor, suma de duraciones de los tests y tiempo total. Mide el conjunto, no una parte, y compara sobre el mismo cÃ³digo.
6. **Nada puede costar en proporciÃ³n a todo lo cargado:** ni una llamada de herramienta ni un test. No uses `CollectGarbage(RF_NoFlags)` en un test (descarga los assets; usa `GARBAGE_COLLECTION_KEEPFLAGS`), no guardes una cachÃ© por tamaÃ±o y fecha de archivo (Unreal da la fecha con precisiÃ³n de un segundo), y usa referencias blandas en el Blueprint del jugador para lo que no haga falta al aparecer.
7. **UE 5.9.0 fijo hasta entregar.** Si algo falla sin que haya cambiado el cÃ³digo, compara primero la versiÃ³n del motor en los dos logs.

Scripts de un solo uso: en un archivo, no en lÃ­nea dentro de un comando; en ASCII o con BOM si son `.ps1` (PowerShell 5.1 lee como ANSI un script sin BOM); y en JavaScript, para reemplazos literales usa `split(a).join(b)`, no `replace(a, b)`.

// Guns (GDD 8): cyberpunk, 3D placeholder viewmodel. Shoot, aim, reload, magazines; the weapon enchant applies.
#pragma once

#include "CoreMinimal.h"
#include "Core/GBPlaceholder.h"
#include "Weapons/GBHeldItem.h"
#include "GBGun.generated.h"

class UStaticMeshComponent;
class UMaterialInstanceDynamic;
class UCanvas;
class UCanvasRenderTarget2D;
struct FGBDamageInfo;
struct FGBGunModelArt;

/** Reload animation shared by the gun viewmodel and the HUD hands: everything is a function of the reload progress (0 to 1). */
namespace GBReloadAnim
{
	/** Where the 2D left hand is, as three 0..1 amounts: Reach (towards the magazine well), Drop (off the bottom of the screen) and Push (the seat bump). */
	struct FHandBeat
	{
		float Reach = 0.f;
		float Drop = 0.f;
		float Push = 0.f;
	};
	/** Zero beats for a negative progress (no reload animation running). */
	FHandBeat HandBeat(float Progress);
	/** 0..1: how far the gun is in its reload pose (eases in over the first beat, out over the last). 0 for a negative progress. */
	float PoseAmount(float Progress);
}

/** What the reload animation shows this frame: the same record for a magazine reload and for one round of a round-by-round reload. */
struct FGBReloadLook
{
	bool bActive = false;
	float Pose = 0.f;   // 0..1 how far the gun is in its reload pose
	float Dip = 0.f;    // 0..1 the dip as a magazine leaves
	float Bump = 0.f;   // 0..1 the seat bump
	float Drop = 0.f;   // 0..1 how far down the magazine / round (and the left hand) is
	float Slide = 0.f;  // 0..1 along the slide path (1 = fully out)
	bool bShowMag = true;
};
			FPlatformMisc::RequestExit(false);
		});
	}

	static bool Tick(float)
	{
		UWorld* World = SmokeWorld.Get();
		if (!World)
		{
			UE_LOG(LogGB, Error, TEXT("SMOKE aborted: the world went away"));
			return false;
		}
		// Read the clock again for every step: a step may wind StartSeconds back (the reload steps stretch the run).
		while (NextStep < Steps.Num() && Steps[NextStep].Time <= static_cast<float>(FPlatformTime::Seconds() - StartSeconds))
		{
			const FStep& Gate = Steps[NextStep];
			if (Gate.MinSimSinceMark > 0.f && World->GetTimeSeconds() - SimMark < Gate.MinSimSinceMark
				&& FPlatformTime::Seconds() - StartSeconds - Gate.Time < SimGateTimeoutSeconds)
			{
				break; // not enough simulated time since the first sample yet: try again next tick
			}
			const FStep& Step = Steps[NextStep++];
			AGBPlayerCharacter* Player = AGBPlayerCharacter::Get(World);
			UE_LOG(LogGB, Display, TEXT("SMOKE step %d/%d (%.1f s): %s"), NextStep, Steps.Num(), Step.Time, Step.Name);
			if (!Player)
			{
				UE_LOG(LogGB, Error, TEXT("SMOKE step skipped: there is no player character"));
				continue;
			}
			Step.Run(World, Player);
		}
		return NextStep < Steps.Num();
	}

	static void Start(const TArray<FString>& Args, UWorld* World)
	{
		if (!World || !World->IsGameWorld())
		{
			UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("gb.Smoke needs a running game world"));
			return;
		}
		BuildSteps();
		if (Args.Num() > 0)
		{
			// Filtered run (gb.Smoke Entity): only the steps whose name starts with the argument, plus the quit step, from 1 s on.
			const FString Prefix = Args[0];
			Steps.RemoveAll([&Prefix](const FStep& Step) { return !FString(Step.Name).StartsWith(Prefix, ESearchCase::IgnoreCase) && FCString::Strcmp(Step.Name, TEXT("quit")) != 0; });
			if (Steps.Num() <= 1)
			{
				UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("gb.Smoke %s: no step starts with that name"), *Prefix);
			}
			float FirstTime = Steps.Num() > 0 ? Steps[0].Time : 0.f;
			for (const FStep& Step : Steps)
			{
				FirstTime = FMath::Min(FirstTime, Step.Time);
			}
			for (FStep& Step : Steps)
			{
				Step.Time -= FirstTime - 1.f;
			}
		}
		NextStep = 0;
		StartSeconds = FPlatformTime::Seconds();
		SmokeWorld = World;
		FTSTicker::GetCoreTicker().RemoveTicker(TickerHandle);
		TickerHandle = FTSTicker::GetCoreTicker().AddTicker(FTickerDelegate::CreateStatic(&Tick));
		UE_LOG(LogGB, Display, TEXT("SMOKE started: %d steps"), Steps.Num());
	}
# Jam dev log (goal: the Glass Box prize for the best dev log)

Everything any agent or person does on the project must be recorded here. If it is not in the dev log, it did not happen as far as the judges are concerned.

**Language:** the Official Rules (7(i)) require every part of the submission in English, and that includes the published dev log. Write `days/*.md`, `media/` captions and `PRE_JAM_DECLARATION.md` in English. Raw logs (`raw/`) and the team's prompts to agents can be in any language. When a day entry quotes a prompt written in Spanish, include the original and an English translation.

## Layout

```
Devlog/
  README.md                  this file (the rules)
  TEMPLATE_DAY.md            template for the daily entry
  PRE_JAM_DECLARATION.md     what was prepared BEFORE Nov 12 (the jam requires declaring it)
  PREJAM_PREP.md             full pre-jam log: timeline, tests, tools evaluated, rules read
  raw/<MACHINE>/             raw automatic logs (NEVER published as they are)
    <YYYY-MM-DD>/claude-<session>.jsonl      every Claude Code event (hook)
    <YYYY-MM-DD>/transcripts/<session>.jsonl full transcript of each session
    <YYYY-MM-DD>/agents.jsonl                every run of an external agent
    <YYYY-MM-DD>/agents/<run>/               prompt, full output and diffstat of that run
    unreal/<log>.jsonl                       agent actions inside the Editor (MCP, Assistant, Canvas)
  days/<YYYY-MM-DD>_DRAFT.md                 automatic draft (already redacted)
  days/<YYYY-MM-DD>.md                       final entry for the day, written by a person (in English)
  media/<YYYY-MM-DD>/                        screenshots, gifs and clips of the day
```

## Rules for everyone (people and agents)

1. **Claude Code** is logged automatically by the **user-level** hooks that `Tools/Devlog/Install-DevlogHooks.ps1` installs on each machine. They log every session opened in the jam root or any subfolder (the root is the folder that contains `Devlog/.jamroot`). Do not disable them and do not delete `.jamroot`.
2. **Any other agent** (Codex, cursor-agent, OpenCode, DeepSeek, Gemini, generation scripts) is ALWAYS launched with:
   `powershell -File Tools/Devlog/Invoke-LoggedAgent.ps1 -Agent <name> -Model <model> -PromptFile <prompt.md> -Purpose "<what for>" -Command '<command>'`
   - Use `-Shell cmd` if the command contains `< file`.
   - That is what records it. An agent launched by hand leaves no record.
   - **Cursor** (the art LAPTOP, which has no repo) is logged by user-level Cursor hooks instead: `Tools/Devlog/Install-CursorHooks.ps1` installs `cursor-devlog-hook.ps1`, which writes `C:\JamLog\raw\<MACHINE>\<date>\cursor-<conversation>.jsonl` (plus transcripts). `Register-LaptopDevlogTasks.ps1` copies that folder every 10 minutes to `Incoming\LAPTOP\_devlog_raw` on CENTRAL, and CENTRAL's `JamDevlogImport` task (`Import-RemoteDevlog.ps1`) moves it into `Devlog/raw/`, where `New-DevlogDay.ps1` reads it. The same kit runs on ASSETS (destination `Incoming\ASSETS\_devlog_raw`).
   - **Codex chats** (desktop app, IDE extension or interactive CLI, i.e. anything not launched through `Invoke-LoggedAgent.ps1`) on machines without the repo: before each sync, `Collect-CodexSessions.ps1` copies the session files Codex keeps in `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\sessions` (full prompts, replies and tool calls) into `C:\JamLog\raw\<MACHINE>\<date>\codex-*.jsonl`. `New-DevlogDay.ps1` reads those too.
   - **Cursor transcripts:** `Collect-CursorTranscripts.ps1` also copies the full agent transcripts Cursor keeps in `%USERPROFILE%.cursorprojects*gent-transcripts`, so chats from before the hook was installed are not lost. The draft takes Cursor prompts from the transcript whenever there is one (it is complete; the hook missed some prompts before the mutex fix).
   - **Claude Code and Gemini CLI** on machines without the repo: `Collect-OtherAgents.ps1` copies their session files (`%USERPROFILE%.claudeprojects`, `%USERPROFILE%.gemini	mp`). OpenCode keeps its sessions in a SQLite database, so it is always launched through `Invoke-LoggedAgent.ps1`; on ASSETS the sync also sends that repo's `Devlog
aw` (`-ExtraSource`).
   - **Size:** every collector strips base64 images (screenshots, renders) from the copies (`JamLogCopy.ps1`): one Codex session on ASSETS went from 110 MB to 4.2 MB with every message and tool call intact. `Import-RemoteDevlog.ps1` refuses files over 50 MB and logs them, because `Devlog/raw` goes to GitHub, which rejects files over 100 MB.
   - On CENTRAL, Codex and OpenCode always run through `Invoke-LoggedAgent.ps1`, so this collection is only needed on machines without the repo.
3. **Prompts as files:** external agents get their prompt in a `.md` under `Prompts/`, never typed loose in the terminal.
4. **Web or desktop tools** (Fab, cloud Canvas, audio or image generators, Blender by hand): in the day entry, write down the tool, the prompt, the result and the final asset, and save a screenshot in `media/`.
5. **Unreal Editor:** always close it with `Tools/Jam/Stop-EditorMcp.ps1`, which closes open asset editors first (an open animation editor crashes the Editor on exit) and collects the log. If it was closed some other way, or before deleting `Saved/`, run `powershell -File Tools/Devlog/Collect-UnrealAgentLog.ps1 -ProjectDir <folder of the .uproject>`.
6. **`JAM_MACHINE` variable:** each machine sets its own (`CENTRAL`, `ASSETS`â€¦) so we know where each thing happened:
   `setx JAM_MACHINE CENTRAL`
7. **Small, frequent commits** with clear messages: the daily draft lists them. Commit `Devlog/raw/` too (the repo is private). Epic may ask for the version control history.
8. **End of day:** run `powershell -File Tools/Devlog/New-DevlogDay.ps1 -Date <YYYY-MM-DD>`. Then a person writes `days/<date>.md` from `TEMPLATE_DAY.md`, starting from the draft.
9. **Publish:** only `days/*.md`, `media/`, `PRE_JAM_DECLARATION.md` and `PREJAM_PREP.md`. Raw logs may contain keys or emails: the draft is already redacted, but check it before publishing. The dev log link goes in the submission, **before the deadline**.
10. **Times in UTC.** The jam ends on Nov 15 at 20:59 EST, which is **01:59 UTC on Nov 16**.

codex
I found three firing/reload edge cases: releasing a click clears the buffered shot, the fire cooldown delays reload cancellation, and a long frame can load several shells before handling a waiting shot. The pistol/rifle paths appear preserved by inspection, and the existing cook directory covers the shotgun meshes. I’m finishing the review of the four new tests.

codex
1. **[P2] [GBGun.h:91](C:/GameJam/Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/Weapons/GBGun.h:91) — Releasing fire discards the buffered shot.** `StopPrimary()` clears `bFireWhenLoaded`, so tapping fire during an empty shotgun reload produces no shot when the shell arrives; the player must hold the button. Preserve that pending shot across ordinary trigger release, and clear it on explicit interruptions such as switching, death, or replacing ammo.

2. **[P2] [GBGun.cpp:1464–1476](C:/GameJam/Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/Weapons/GBGun.cpp:1464) — Reload cancellation happens after the fire-cooldown gate.** The base `UpdateTrigger()` checks `IsAttackReady()` before calling `PerformAttack()`, so pressing fire shortly after shooting leaves the reload running until the cooldown expires. Handle the shotgun’s reload-interruption request when fire is pressed, then retain the shot until normal firing rules permit it; magazine guns can retain their existing path.

3. **[P2] [GBGun.cpp:1420–1428](C:/GameJam/Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/Weapons/GBGun.cpp:1420) — A waiting shot is handled only after every elapsed shell cycle.** `Advance()` can load several shells during a hitch before `bFireWhenLoaded` is examined, despite the requirement to interrupt at the first available shell. The player can emerge from the hitch with extra shells loaded and the reload stopped, instead of firing the first shell and entering the normal empty-gun reload; allow stepping to yield after that first shell and process the pending shot before subsequent cycles.

4. **[P2] [GBGun.cpp:830–835](C:/GameJam/Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/Weapons/GBGun.cpp:830) — Cancelling early makes the hands jump toward the full reload pose.** The root blends from its captured partial transform, but `CurrentPoseAmount = Left` starts the hands near **1**, regardless of the pose amount at cancellation. Capture the starting pose amount in `BeginReloadBlend()` and multiply it by `Left`, so the hands follow the same blend as the gun.

5. **[P3] [GBWeaponsTests.cpp:61–165](C:/GameJam/Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/Tests/GBWeaponsTests.cpp:61) — The four new tests leave specific timing errors undetected.** These wrong implementations would still pass their respective tests, allowing reload timing regressions to reach play:

   **`PerRoundFullReload`:** stretching the total duration to **2.02 seconds** passes because the final sample is actually at **2.03**, after the previous sample at 1.99. Advance exactly to 2.00 and assert completion there.

   **`PerRoundShortReserve`:** calculating the cycle as `ReloadTime / Planned` passes because the only advancing sample is ten seconds long. Check immediately before and at `ReloadTime / MagazineSize`.

   **`PerRoundLongFrame`:** storing `Fmod(DeltaSeconds, CycleSeconds)` instead of the accumulated remainder passes both calls: the second call leaves an incorrect 0.7 cycle instead of 0.1, which is never checked. Assert the second remainder and reserve count.

   **`PerRoundStopPartWay`:** dividing reload time by the initial empty space—five here—instead of capacity still moves two rounds during the sampled interval and passes every assertion. Check the first shell’s boundary before testing cancellation.

I would fix trigger interruption and buffering first. By static inspection, reserve conversion, pickups, inventory, stored guns, era/respawn refills, zero-value division guards, switch cleanup, and mesh cook coverage look solid; the pistol/rifle reload, display, kick, trigger, glow, and energy-bar paths appear preserved. No files were modified, and no builds, game sessions, or tests were run.
